NASA-STD-6001B w/CHANGE 3


In the mechanical impact test, reaction normally takes the form of ignition or oxidation. A
reaction from mechanical impact can be determined by an audible report, an electronically or
visually detected flash, or detectable charring of the specimen, specimen cup, or striker pin.


# A.2.5.5 Test Conditions


a. [FOCR 278] For ambient pressure LOX tests, the test conditions (pressure and


temperature) shall be the test facility ambient pressure and the LOX boiling point at that
pressure.


b. [FOCR 279] For variable pressure LOX and GOX tests, the test conditions (pressure


and temperature) shall be determined for each test as recommended in the OCA.


# A.2.5.6 Specimen Preparation


a. [FOCR 280] Materials shall be prepared as stated in ASTM G86, Standard Test


Method for Determining Ignition Sensitivity of Materials to Mechanical Impact in Ambient
Liquid Oxygen and Pressurized Liquid and Gaseous Oxygen Environments.


b. [FOCR 281] The test material dimensions specified in ASTM G86 shall be used,


unless otherwise specified by the responsible NASA program/project M&P organization.


As a minimum, unless otherwise specified or approved by the NASA program/project M&P
organization, contamination on the surfaces of solid, nonporous specimens should be removed
by washing with deionized water and any detergent, rinsing with deionized water, and drying
with filtered GN2.


Particulates on the surfaces of solid, porous specimens should be removed with filtered GN2.


# c. [FOCR 282] The specimen shall be inspected and flaws noted.


# d. [FOCR 283] If the flaws result from specimen preparation at the test facility, new specimens shall be prepared.


If the flaws do not result from specimen preparation at the test facility, the customer is contacted
to verify that the test should proceed.


e. [FOCR 284] When testing materials to determine relative ranking, specimen


preparation (including cleaning) and condition (for example, thickness, surface finish, product
form) shall be identical.


# A.2.5.7 Test System


[FOCR 285] The test system shall be identical to that described in ASTM G86.

# A.2.6 Gaseous Fluid Impact for Components


# A.2.6.1 Purpose


This test provides supplemental information on the reaction sensitivity of components to rapid
pressurization heating when pneumatically impacted in a pressurized GOX system.


The test can be performed to meet any of the following objectives:


1. To characterize the reaction sensitivity of a component at a specified pressure.

2. To determine the pressure threshold for ignition of a component over a range of


pressures.


c. To determine the temperature reaction threshold of a component at a specified pressure.

# A.2.6.2 Test Summary


# a. [FOCR 291] The test specimens shall be components in their normal use condition, including the end-use cleanliness level.


b. [FOCR 292] The test specimens shall be subjected to gaseous fluid impact at 1.25


times Maximum Operating Pressure (MOP), followed by functional tests and inspection for
evidence of ignition.


# A.2.6.3 Test Criteria


This test evaluates the reaction sensitivity of components when pneumatically impacted in a
pressurized GOX system. Therefore, the test criteria are dependent on the application.


# a. [FOCR 293] At least one component shall be subjected to a minimum of 60 pneumatic impacts.


# b. [FOCR 294] Pressurization shall be through the ports specified by an OCA.


Testing of up to three components is preferable.


# A.2.6.4 Definitions


The definition of the relevant term used in the description of this test method is:


Gaseous fluid impact: Heat delivered to a specimen by rapid fluid compression; may also
be referred to as pneumatic impact, rapid pressurization, or adiabatic compression.


# A.2.6.5 Test Conditions


# a. [FOCR 295] Test pressure shall be 1.25 times MOP.


# b. [FOCR 296] Test gas temperature shall be 60 (±3) °C (140 (±5.4) °F).


c. [FOCR 297] Pressurization rate to within 95 percent of the test pressure shall be 20


(+0/-5) msec to enhance reaction probability in the component.


# A.2.6.6 Specimen Preparation


[FOCR 298] Components shall be tested in their normal use condition, including the end-use
cleanliness level.

# A.2.7.1 Purpose


The purpose of this test is to identify changes resulting from incidental exposure (minor amounts,
such as a splash) to fluids that degrade or penetrate the material or produce a reaction.

# A.2.7.2 Test Criteria


This test is used to determine and document the effects of the hydrazine family of fuels, nitrogen
tetroxide and mixed oxides of nitrogen, and ammonia on materials; therefore, any test criteria
are dependent on the intended use of the material.


The acceptability of the material is determined by the test requester and the responsible NASA
program/project M&P organization.


# A.2.7.3 Definitions


Definitions of relevant terms used in the description of this test method are:


Degradation: An adverse physical or chemical change in a substance.
Reaction: A chemical change in which a substance decomposes, combines with other
substances, or interchanges constituents with other substances.


# A.2.7.4 Test Conditions


# a. [FOCR 303] Unless otherwise specified, the test conditions shall be the test facility ambient temperature and pressure.


Incidental exposure time is considered to be ≤240 min.


Test exposure time is specified by the test requester, based on the intended use for the material
and expected duration of field exposure.


In the case of gross reactivity, the test may be terminated early.


b. Test Fluids


(1) [FOCR 304] Hydrazine shall meet the requirements of MIL-PRF-26536.


1. [FOCR 305] UDMH shall meet the requirements of MIL-PRF-25604.

2. [FOCR 306] MMH shall meet the requirements of MIL-PRF-27404.

3. [FOCR 307] Aerozine 50 shall meet the requirements of MIL-PRF-27402.

4. [FOCR 308] Nitrogen tetroxide and mixed oxides of nitrogen shall meet the requirements of MIL-PRF-26539.

5. [FOCR 309] Ammonia shall be procured with a stated purity of 99.99 percent (liquid phase).

# A.2.7.5 Specimen Preparation


# A.2.7.5.1 Films, Fabrics, Sheets, Metals, and Composites


Preparation of these specimens for testing involves the following tasks:


a. [FOCR 310] Preparation of a specimen of the proper mass or dimensions: specimens


shall be prepared to a geometric surface area of 10.2 by 10.2 cm (4 by 4 in).


# b. [FOCR 311] Specimens shall be cleaned and dried to end-use specifications.


# A.2.7.5.2 Adhesives and Coatings


[FOCR 312] Adhesives and coatings shall be applied in a thickness equivalent to normal use on
aluminum foil or 304L stainless steel substrate and cured, if necessary, in accordance with the
manufacturer's instructions.


# A.2.7.5.3 Tapes


[FOCR 313] Tapes shall be applied in the as-received condition and thickness on aluminum foil
or 304L stainless steel substrate or a nonreactive substrate, e.g., watch glass, glass Petri dish.


# A.2.7.5.4 Greases and Gels


[FOCR 314] Greases and gels shall be applied on aluminum foil or 304L stainless steel substrate
in a thickness equivalent to the normal use and cured, if required, in accordance with the
manufacturer's instructions.


# A.2.7.5.5 Complex Shapes


[FOCR 315] Complex shapes, e.g., O-rings, cables, and pipes, shall be tested in a cleanliness and
configuration consistent with the intended use.


# A.2.7.6 Test System


This test requires handling hazardous fluids and has the potential to produce energetic events
such as fire, thermal runaway, and explosion.


a. [FOCR 316] The test system for the reactivity and penetration test for films, fabrics,


sheets, metals, adhesives, coatings, and composites shall consist of a nonreactive glass beaker in
a fume hood approved for the handling of the test fluid.

b. [FOCR 317] The test system for the reactivity test for all other materials shall consist

# A.2.7.7 Pre-Test Procedure


a. [FOCR 318] The fluid to be used in testing shall be analyzed and verified to meet the

b. [FOCR 319] All pertinent information for the test (for example, specimen


identification, composition, pre-test mass and dimensions, and fluid analysis results) shall be

# d. [FOCR 321] The test system shall be visibly clean.


# e. [FOCR 322] All the measuring devices shall be in current calibration.


f. [FOCR 323] As a precaution, initial exposure test of the specimen material shall be

# A.2.7.8 Test Procedure


# A.2.7.8.1 Reactivity and Penetration of Films, Fabrics, Sheets, Metals, and Composites


# a. [FOCR 324] The test material specimen shall be placed over a nonreactive beaker.


Optionally, position a sheathed thermocouple or resistance thermometer so that it touches the
center of the test specimen and will be immersed in the test fluid.


b. [FOCR 325] The test fluid (typically 1 ml total) shall be added drop by drop, slowly


at first, and observed for any evidence of gross reactivity.


Optionally, monitor the temperature of the test fluid.


c. [FOCR 326] If no gross reactivity is observed, the remainder of the test fluid shall be


added to the center of the specimen, without exposing the edges of the specimen to the fluid.


During specimen preparation, consideration should be given to edge effects of the specimen,


e.g., cut ends of composite structures, insufficient application of coatings.

NASA-STD-6001B w/CHANGE 3


Specimens are prepared to a geometric surface area; however, actual surface area may be
greater.


# d. [FOCR 327] The specimen shall be continuously exposed to the test fluid for the specified exposure time.


Test fluid should be added as required to maintain a visible liquid film on the test specimen
during the exposure time.


Optionally, monitor the temperature of the test fluid throughout the specified exposure time.


e. [FOCR 328] The specimen shall be observed for visible signs of reaction (table 4)


(for example, burning, smoking, frothing, charring, solubility, swelling, fracture, or penetration
of the specimen) throughout the test.


If the temperature was monitored, the starting temperature of the test fluid, the maximum fluid
temperature observed during the test and the time of occurrence, and the test fluid temperature
at the end of the test are recorded.


The test may be terminated at signs of gross reactivity.


# f. [FOCR 329] After testing, each component shall be visually inspected for signs of degradation.


g. [FOCR 330] The time of the first instance of penetration (first drop of liquid in the


beaker) shall be recorded.


h. [FOCR 331] For materials used for protective garments, the time of initial appearance


of wetness on the underside of the test specimen shall be recorded.


Atmospheric condensation could form on the underside of the test specimen, giving a false
indication of penetration; verification of penetration should be made.


# A.2.7.8.2 Reactivity of All Other Materials


# a. [FOCR 332] The specimen shall be placed on a nonreactive substrate, e.g., watch glass, glass Petri dish.


Optionally, position a sheathed thermocouple or resistance thermometer so that it touches the
center of the test specimen and will be immersed in the test fluid.


b. [FOCR 333] The test fluid shall be added drop by drop, slowly at first, and observed


for any evidence of gross reactivity.

Optionally, monitor the temperature of the test fluid.

# A.2.8 Autogenous Ignition Temperature


# A.2.8.1 Purpose


The purpose of this test is to provide information on the temperature at which liquids or solids
will ignite spontaneously in elevated-pressure oxygen environments. The test method is designed
for high-pressure GOX but may be used in atmospheres from 0.5 percent to 100 percent oxygen.


# A.2.8.2 Test Summary


The autogenous ignition temperature test consists of exposing a material in a reaction vessel to a
pressurized environment containing 0.5 percent to 100 percent oxygen and heating at a
predetermined rate. The minimum temperature required to cause the specimen to ignite


APPROVED FOR PUBLIC


RELEASE-DISTRIBUTION IS UNLIMITED


81 of 158




NASA-STD-6001B w/CHANGE 3


spontaneously is denoted by a sudden temperature rise and is considered the autogenous ignition
temperature.


# A.2.8.3 Test Criteria


This test is used to determine the autogenous ignition temperature (commonly called the
autoignition temperature) under specified conditions of the test; therefore, any test criteria are
dependent upon the intended use of the material, as evaluated through hazards analyses through
engineering assessments for safe system operations.


This procedure provides a numerical value for the temperature at the onset of ignition under
controlled conditions. This is only one of a number of properties to be considered in assessing
the overall ignitibility hazard of a material.


# A.2.8.4 Definition


The definition of the relevant term used in the description of this test method is:


Autogenous ignition temperature: The lowest temperature at which a material will
spontaneously ignite under specific test conditions.


# A.2.8.5 Test Conditions


[FOCR 351] The test conditions shall be identical to those described in ASTM G72, Standard
Test Method for Autogenous Ignition Temperature of Liquids and Solids in a High-Pressure
Oxygen-Enriched Environment.


# A.2.8.6 Test Systems and Procedures


# a. [FOCR 352] The test systems and procedures shall be identical to those described in ASTM G72.


# b. [FOCR 353] At least five specimens shall be tested under each test condition.


# A.2.8.7 Reporting


a. [FOCR 354] The test report shall include specimen identification, the method used,


test conditions, ignition temperature, temperature rise on ignition, and observations from the test.


# b. [FOCR 355] The test report shall be submitted to MAPTIS in a MAPTIS-compatible format.


# A.2.8.8 Precision


[FOCR 356] Measurements shall meet the ASTM G72 precision requirements.


APPROVED FOR PUBLIC


RELEASE-DISTRIBUTION IS UNLIMITED

NASA-STD-6001B w/CHANGE 3


# A.2.9 Heat of Combustion


# A.2.9.1 Purpose


The purpose of this test is to provide information on the heat evolved per unit mass when a
polymeric material is completely burned in GOX.


# A.2.9.2 Test Summary


Heat of combustion is determined by burning a weighed specimen in a GOX environment in a
bomb calorimeter under controlled conditions. The heat of combustion is calculated from
temperature measurements before, during, and after combustion.


# A.2.9.3 Test Criteria


This test is used to determine the heat of combustion of nonmetals. Any test criteria are
dependent upon the intended use of the material, as evaluated through hazards analyses by
engineering assessments to ensure safe systems operations. The heat of combustion is only one of
a number of properties to be considered in assessing the overall oxygen compatibility of a
material.


# A.2.9.4 Definitions


Definitions of relevant terms used in the description of this test method are:


Gross heat of combustion: The quantity of heat released when a unit mass of fuel is
burned in a constant volume enclosure, with the products being gaseous, other than water that is
condensed to the liquid state.


Net heat of combustion: The quantity of heat released when a unit mass of fuel is burned
at constant pressure, with all of the products, including water, being gaseous.


# A.2.9.5 Test Conditions


[FOCR 357] The test conditions shall be identical to those described in ASTM D240, Standard
Test Method for Heat of Combustion of Liquid Hydrocarbon Fuels by Bomb Calorimeter, or in
ASTM D4809, Standard Test Method for Heat of Combustion of Liquid Hydrocarbon Fuels by
Bomb Calorimeter (Precision Method).


# A.2.9.6 Test Systems and Procedures


[FOCR 358] The test systems and procedures shall be identical to those described in ASTM
D240 or ASTM D4809.


APPROVED FOR PUBLIC


RELEASE-DISTRIBUTION IS UNLIMITED


83 of 158




NASA-STD-6001B w/CHANGE 3


The gross heat of combustion is determined experimentally; however, the net heat of combustion
is the quantity more commonly used. The gross heat of combustion for polymers of interest
(especially oxygen systems) is conservative when compared with the net heat of combustion by
~0.6 MJ/kg (~150 cal/g) or less. The gross heat of combustion is adequate for evaluating
materials compatibility for oxygen compatibility applications.


# A.2.9.7 Reporting


a. [FOCR 359] The test report shall include specimen identification, the method used,


test conditions, gross heat of combustion, and observations from the test.


# b. [FOCR 360] The test report shall be submitted to MAPTIS in a MAPTIS-compatible format.


# A.2.9.8 Precision


[FOCR 361] Measurements shall meet the ASTM D240 or ASTM D4809 precision
requirements.


# A.2.10 Oxygen Index


# A.2.10.1 Purpose


The purpose of this test is to provide information on the relative flammability of materials. It is a
useful screen for determining whether previously untested nonmetallic materials are likely to be
acceptable for use in oxygen systems, but its results cannot be used for absolute prediction of
oxygen compatibility.


# A.2.10.2 Test Summary


The oxygen index is determined by burning the material in a candle-like configuration and
varying the oxygen concentration to find the lowest oxygen concentration that will support
combustion.


# A.2.10.3 Test Criteria


This test is used to determine the oxygen index of nonmetals. Any test criteria are dependent
upon the intended use of the material, as evaluated through hazards analyses by engineering
assessments to ensure safe systems operations. The oxygen index is only one of a number of
properties to be considered in assessing the overall oxygen compatibility of a material.


# A.2.10.4 Definitions


The definition of the relevant term used in the description of this test method is:

RELEASE-DISTRIBUTION IS UNLIMITED

NASA-STD-6001B w/CHANGE 3


Oxygen index: The minimum concentration of oxygen, expressed as a volume percent,
in a mixture of oxygen and nitrogen that will just support flaming combustion of a material
initially at room temperature under the conditions of ASTM D2863, Standard Test Method for
Measuring the Minimum Oxygen Concentration to Support Candle-Like Combustion of Plastics
(Oxygen Index).


# A.2.10.5 Test Conditions


[FOCR 362] The test conditions shall be identical to those described in ASTM G125, Standard
Test Method for Measuring Liquid and Solid Material Fire Limits in Gaseous Oxidants, or
ASTM D2863.


# A.2.10.6 Test Systems and Procedures


[FOCR 363] The test systems and procedures shall be identical to those described in ASTM
G125 or ASTM D2863.


# A.2.10.7 Reporting


# a. [FOCR 364] The test report shall comply with ASTM G125 or ASTM D2863.


# b. [FOCR 365] The test report shall be submitted to MAPTIS in a MAPTIS-compatible format.


# A.2.11 Electrical Arc Test


# A.2.11.1 Purpose


Electrical arc tests are designed to determine the voltage and current needed to produce ignition
in polymeric materials.


# A.2.11.2 Test Criteria


[FOCR 366] A material shall pass at the test conditions if there is no ignition in 60 tests.


Typically, the test current is varied to determine the threshold for ignition of the material.


# A.2.11.3 Test Conditions


[FOCR 367] The following test conditions shall be determined by the OCA or by the test requester:
test pressure, oxygen concentration, test specimen material/configuration, voltage, and current.


# A.2.11.4 Specimen Preparation


[FOCR 368] The test specimen configuration shall be specified in the OCA or by the test
requester.


APPROVED FOR PUBLIC RELEASE-DISTRIBUTION IS UNLIMITED


85 of 158




NASA-STD-6001B w/CHANGE 3


This preparation may include fraying the test materials with a noncontaminating wire brush.


# A.2.11.5 Test System


Electrical arc tests are performed in a chamber pressurized with GOX or a specified gas
mixture. The apparatus includes a single strand of wire in contact with the test material (figure
5, Electrical Arc Test Apparatus). The test chamber accommodates a gas inlet and outlet, a view
window for normal and high-speed video recording, a test specimen mounting block (with built-
in heat exchanger for temperature control), a thermocouple for temperature measurement, and
power for the electrical arcing. The system is capable of a maximum test pressure of 689 kPa


(100 psia). The power supply is designed to simulate batteries, while allowing flexibility for


different voltages and currents during testing.


Figure 5-Electrical Arc Test Apparatus


# A.2.11.6 Test Procedure


a. Before testing begins, the test voltage is set to correspond to the predetermined test


conditions, and the current is set slightly lower than the desired test conditions.


b. The appropriate wire size is determined, and the wire is clamped into place.


APPROVED FOR PUBLIC


RELEASE-DISTRIBUTION IS UNLIMITED


86 of 158




NASA-STD-6001B w/CHANGE 3


c. The test chamber is sealed, purged, and pressurized with the specified pressure and


oxygen concentration.


d. Power is then applied to the wire, and the test conductor manually increases the


current until the wire breaks, creating an arcing event.


1. If an ignition occurs, testing is continued at a lower current level.

2. If no ignition occurs, high-speed video and visual inspection are used to verify that


the wire broke in the desired location and was in contact with the test specimen. Each test
consists of one wire-break event, which is observed for visual evidence of test material ignition.


# A.2.11.7 Reporting


a. [FOCR 369] The test report shall include specimen material and configuration, test


pressure, oxygen concentration, and current/voltage required for ignition.


# b. [FOCR 370] The test report shall be submitted to MAPTIS in a MAPTIS-compatible format.


# A.2.12 Frictional Heating


# A.2.12.1 Purpose


The GOX and LOX frictional heating tests are designed to evaluate a material's susceptibility to
ignition by frictional heating. These tests include rotational friction tests and reciprocal friction
tests. Frictional heating tests can be used to accomplish the following objectives:


a. To assess the ability of a pair of materials to convert mechanical energy to thermal


energy during rubbing.


1. To determine the coefficient of friction and wear.

2. For rotational friction tests, to determine the minimum contact pressure (P) required


to ignite a test material specimen at a given linear surface velocity (v). This product (Pvign) is
used to rank the relative ignition resistance of materials.


d. For reciprocal friction tests, to determine whether the materials will ignite at the test


frequency profile and displacement of movement.


# A.2.12.2 Test Criteria


[FOCR 371] The test criteria shall be defined in the OCA.


APPROVED FOR PUBLIC


RELEASE-DISTRIBUTION IS UNLIMITED

NASA-STD-6001B w/CHANGE 3


For both rotational and reciprocal friction tests, ignition is detected typically by a significant
and rapid temperature increase, displacement, or a change in pressure (an initial pressure drop
followed by a pressure increase). After termination of the test, the test specimens are inspected
visually for consumed material.


# A.2.12.3 Definitions


Definitions of relevant terms used in the description of this test method are:


1. : Minimum contact pressure.


v: Linear surface velocity.


# A.2.12.4 Test Conditions


[FOCR 372] The test parameter values shall be determined by the OCA or by the test requester.


The test parameters for frictional heating tests are pressure, oxygen concentration (for GOX
tests), rotational speed (for rotational friction tests), frequency profile and displacement of
movement (for reciprocal friction tests), normal force, and test specimen materials.


Most of the existing friction data was generated using rotational friction at the following
standard set of conditions, which may be useful for comparing data:


a. The oxygen pressure is maintained constant at 6.9 MPa (1000 psig) for GOX tests


and 2.1 MPa (300 psig) for LOX tests.


1. The rotational speed is maintained constant at 17,000 revolutions per minute (rpm).

2. The normal load is increased from 0 at a rate of 1.6 to 1.7 N/sec (7 to 7.5 lbf/sec) to


the point of specimen failure or to the maximum load capacity of the test system.


Any of these test conditions may be changed to meet the recommendations in the OCA.


# A.2.12.5 Specimen Preparation


# a. [FOCR 373] The test specimen configuration shall be specified in the OCA or by the test requester.


b. [FOCR 374] Before testing, the specimens shall be prepared according to the end-use


application or by washing with any warm liquid detergent solution, rinsing with deionized water,
and drying with GN2.


# c. [FOCR 375] The specimens shall then be sealed in clean bags until test.

## d. [FOCR 376] From this point, the specimens shall be handled with clean, powder-free, lint-free gloves to maintain cleanliness.


Most of the existing rotational friction data was generated with specimens configured as follows,
which may be useful for comparing data:


For each rotational friction test, one test specimen remained stationary and one specimen
rotated. The test apparatus was configured as shown in figure 6, Frictional Heating Test
System. Figure 7, Rotational Frictional Heating Stationary Test Specimen, shows test specimen
configuration. The stationary test specimen had two thermocouple holes
(0.1 cm (0.039 in) in diameter) on its radial side. The rotational test specimens were fabricated
similarly but without the 0.1-cm (0.039-in) diameter holes.

Heating Test System

Figure 7-Rotational Frictional Heating Stationary Test Specimen
(all dimensions in inches)


# A.2.12.6 Test System


The test system varies, depending on which test is used and where the test is performed.


Most of the existing rotational friction data was generated using an apparatus similar to that
shown in figures 6 and 8, High-Pressure Test Chamber. This apparatus consists of a high-
pressure test chamber, an electrical motor, a transmission assembly, and a pneumatic
actuation cylinder. The chamber contains a rotating shaft that extends through the chamber
by a series of bearings and seals. The shaft is connected at one end to a drive
motor/transmission assembly that is capable of rotating the shaft at rotational speeds from
1000 to 27,000 rpm. The other end of the shaft is connected to a pneumatically actuated
cylinder that allows axial movement of the shaft and provides the capability of applying a
normal load of up to 4450 N (1000 lbf) to the test specimen. The rotating test specimen is
mounted on the shaft, and the stationary test specimen is affixed to the test chamber.


The rotating test specimen has an average linear surface velocity, relative to the stationary
specimen, of approximately 20.5 m/sec (67.2 ft/sec) when the shaft rotates at 17,000 rpm.

The pre-test and test procedures vary, depending on which test is used and where the test is

# A.2.12.8 Reporting


a. [FOCR 377] Results of frictional heating tests shall be summarized in a report that


includes documentation of the specimen materials and configuration, the test conditions and
apparatus, and test results.


# b. [FOCR 378] If the coefficient of friction is calculated from the data, this value shall be reported.

# A.2.13 Particle Impact Test


# A.2.13.1 Purpose


Particle impact tests are designed to evaluate the susceptibility of materials or components to
ignition.


Particle impact tests can be used to accomplish the following objectives:


a. To determine the conditions at which ignition and consumption of a material occurs


when impacted by single or multiple particles entrained in a flow of GOX.


b. To determine if a specific material or component is subject to ignition and sustained


combustion in a given flow environment when impacted by single or multiple particles entrained in a
flow of GOX.


# A.2.13.2 Test Criteria


# a. [FOCR 381] The test criteria shall be defined in the OCA or by the test requester.


The following criteria are typically applied:


For testing materials:


No ignition: Characterized by no evidence of combustion. A material that does not
ignite may show one or more particle indentations on the surface. These
indentations may include localized erosion.


Ignition: A portion of or the entire specimen is consumed, and the target is not
recoverable.


For testing components:


No ignition: Characterized by no evidence of combustion. A component that does
not ignite may show one or more particle indentations on the inside surface of the
component. These indentations may include localized erosion.


Ignition: Characterized by obvious consumption of the component, which may
include burnout.


Typically for materials, the test conditions are varied to determine the threshold for ignition.


b. [FOCR 382] For component tests, a minimum of 60 tests shall be performed at a


single set of conditions on a single component.


APPROVED FOR PUBLIC


RELEASE-DISTRIBUTION IS UNLIMITED


92 of 158




NASA-STD-6001B w/CHANGE 3


1. [FOCR 383] If there are no ignitions in 60 tests, the component shall pass the test at the test conditions.

2. [FOCR 384] One ignition in 60 tests shall constitute failure of the test at the test conditions.


# A.2.13.3 Definitions


Definitions of relevant terms used in the description of this test method are:


High-velocity particle impact: Tests are performed using the supersonic particle impact
test fixture. Gas stream velocities in this test system are supersonic (>Mach 1). Gas velocities
vary with the temperature and pressure of the GOX system.


Low-velocity particle impact: Tests are performed with the subsonic particle impact test
fixture. Gas stream velocities in this system are up to 180 m/sec (600 ft/sec).


# A.2.13.4 Test Conditions


a. [FOCR 385] The following test conditions shall be determined by the OCA or by the


test requester: gas/target temperature, target pressure, gas velocity, target material/configuration,
and particle configuration (size, material, and quantity).


b. [FOCR 386] Components shall be tested at the worst-case conditions as determined


by the OCA or by the test requester.


Typically, material tests are performed with 2000-μm (0.08-in) diameter aluminum 2017
particles at supersonic velocity and an upstream test pressure of 27.6 (±0.7) MPa
(4000 (±100) psig). These conditions may be considered the standard test parameters with
which to rank materials against each other for resistance to ignition by particle impact. The
majority of the supersonic data generated was generated at this standard set of conditions.


# A.2.13.5 Specimen Preparation


# a. [FOCR 387] The test specimen configuration shall be specified in the OCA or by the test requester.


b. [FOCR 388] Before testing, material specimens shall be prepared according to the


end-use conditions or by washing with any liquid detergent solution, rinsing with deionized
water, and drying with GN2.


# c. [FOCR 389] The specimens shall then be sealed in clean bags until testing.


# d. [FOCR 390] From this point on, the targets shall be handled with powder-free, lint- free gloves to maintain cleanliness.


APPROVED FOR PUBLIC


RELEASE-DISTRIBUTION IS UNLIMITED


93 of 158




NASA-STD-6001B w/CHANGE 3


e. [FOCR 391] The configurations of test targets for the low- and high-velocity testing


shall be as follows:


1. Material Specimens.


A. High-Velocity Particle Impact Material Specimen Preparation (figure 9, High-
Velocity Particle Impact Target Specimen Configuration):


Specimens are cup-shaped with an outside diameter of 0.99 cm
(0.390 in) and a target surface thickness of 1.5 cm (0.060 in). Each specimen
is press fit onto a copper specimen holder for testing.


Figure 9-High-Velocity Particle Impact Target Specimen Configuration
(all dimensions are in inches)


B. Low-Velocity Particle Impact Material Specimen Preparation (figure 10,
Low-Velocity Particle Impact Target Specimen Configuration):


Specimens are flat discs, 1.5 cm (0.060 in) thick, with holes drilled radially to
allow flow through the outer edge of the target.


APPROVED FOR PUBLIC


RELEASE-DISTRIBUTION IS UNLIMITED


94 of 158




NASA-STD-6001B w/CHANGE 3


Figure 10-Low-Velocity Particle Impact Target Specimen Configuration
(all dimensions are in inches)


(2) Component Testing: Components shall be configured in their end-use
configuration.


Components can be adapted to either the high- or low-velocity test systems.


# A.2.13.6 Test System


a. [FOCR 392] Particle impact tests shall be conducted in a facility capable of supplying


heated GOX at the desired test pressure.


b. [FOCR 393] High-velocity particle impact test system: The test specimen shall be


placed on a mounting post directly in line with the gas flow stream.


The high-velocity particle impact test system (figure 11, High-Velocity Particle Impact Test
System) consists of four major sections: the particle injector assembly, gas inlet and flow
straightener, converging-diverging nozzle, and specimen holder. GOX enters the system at
subsonic velocities. As the gas enters the converging portion of the nozzle and continues into the
throat, it accelerates to a velocity of ~Mach 1. The gas expands as it enters the diverging
portion of the nozzle and accelerates to supersonic velocities. The gas stream impinges on the
specimen face, creating a stagnation pressure, and flows around the specimen. (This stagnation
pressure is lower than the upstream pressure.)


APPROVED FOR PUBLIC


RELEASE-DISTRIBUTION IS UNLIMITED


95 of 158




NASA-STD-6001B w/CHANGE 3


Figure 11-High-Velocity Particle Impact Test System


The following describes testing steps for an example test facility configuration; other facility
configurations may lend themselves to variations of this description:


1. A bare wire thermocouple is sandwiched between the back face of the test specimen and the specimen post.

2. The post is placed in a quick-change holder, which mounts on the outlet of the convergent-divergent nozzle.

3. The upstream GOX pressure is measured by two bonded strain-gauge pressure transducers located upstream of the inlet.

4. The flow stream (GOX) temperature is measured by a Type-T thermocouple located in approximately the same location.

5. The bare wire thermocouple, mounted behind the specimen, measures the test specimen temperature. This temperature is reported as the test temperature.


c. Low-velocity particle impact test system:


APPROVED FOR PUBLIC


RELEASE-DISTRIBUTION IS UNLIMITED


96 of 158




NASA-STD-6001B w/CHANGE 3


The low velocity test system delivers GOX at velocities up to 183 m/sec (600 ft/sec), and it
consists of the particle injector, the flow straightener, and the impact chamber (figure 12, Low-
Velocity Particle Impact Test System).


Figure 12-Low-Velocity Particle Impact Test System


1. [FOCR 394] A flow orifice, sized to produce the desired gas velocity, shall be placed immediately downstream of either the material specimen or component.

2. [FOCR 395] GOX shall enter the particulate injector at subsonic velocities and flow through the flow straightener section and around the target specimen in the impact chamber.


The following describes testing steps for an example test facility configuration; other facility
configurations may lend themselves to variations of this description.


1. The upstream GOX pressure is measured by a bonded strain-gauge pressure transducer mounted in the flow straightener of the test fixture, located upstream of the inlet.

2. The flow stream (GOX) temperature is measured by a Type-T thermocouple located in approximately the same location. This temperature is reported as the test temperature.


# A.2.13.7 Test Procedure


The following steps describe the testing procedure for an example test facility configuration;
other facility configurations may lend themselves to variations of this description:


a. Particles are loaded into the particle injector, and the test material or component is


configured for testing.


b. A high-speed video camera is recommended to record the test.


APPROVED FOR PUBLIC


RELEASE-DISTRIBUTION IS UNLIMITED


97 of 158




NASA-STD-6001B w/CHANGE 3


c. GOX at the desired test pressure and temperature is allowed to flow until the


temperature of the target specimen or component is achieved and the gas flow stabilizes.


1. The particle(s) are injected into the flow stream.

2. After impact, the GOX flow is terminated, and the test system is allowed to vent to


ambient pressure.


f. To verify that particle impacts have occurred, the presence of a flash is noted, and the


target specimen or component is inspected visually.


g. At the completion of test data storage and specimen inspection, the procedure is


repeated until the specimen has either passed or failed testing.


# A.2.13.8 Reporting


a. [FOCR 396] Results of particle impact tests shall be summarized in a report that


includes documentation of the specimen target material or component, the particle material and
size, the test conditions, and the response of each target to impact.


# b. [FOCR 397] Pre- and post-test photographic images of the test specimens shall be included in the report.


# c. [FOCR 398] The test report shall be submitted to MAPTIS in a MAPTIS-compatible format.

# APPENDIX B


# CERTIFICATION OF FACILITIES


# B.1 Purpose and/or Scope


The purpose of this Appendix is to provide requirements for certification of facilities.


# B.2 [FOCR 399] Certification of test facilities shall be obtained for the performance of any of


the required tests included in this document.


Certification is necessary because data from these tests will be presented for materials selection
approval.


# B.3 [FOCR 400] Certification shall be the responsibility of a panel appointed by the NASA


Office of the Chief Engineer.


# B.4 [FOCR 401] The certification panel shall consist of M&P representatives from NASA-


certified facilities and be supported by specialists in appropriate disciplines to evaluate specific
test methods.


# B.5 [FOCR 402] An application for certification shall be submitted according to requirements


listed below:


a. [FOCR 403] The test facility applying for certification shall prepare and submit


detailed written procedures to perform all aspects of the subject test methods to the certification
panel for review.


b. [FOCR 404] Procedures shall include provisions for instrument calibration, specimen


preparation, test chamber certification, test atmosphere analysis or verification, data analysis,
report preparation, quality control provisions, recording and archiving of test data, materials
control, and control of flight articles.


c. [FOCR 405] The test facility procedures shall also include the definition of the


minimum acceptable personnel qualifications, training requirements, and personnel certification
procedures.


# d. [FOCR 406] Changes to these procedures shall be maintained and made available at the request of the certification panel.


e. [FOCR 407] The test facility also shall supply documentation demonstrating that


operations performed in the subject test methods present no hazards to personnel or flight
hardware.


APPROVED FOR PUBLIC


RELEASE-DISTRIBUTION IS UNLIMITED


99 of 158




NASA-STD-6001B w/CHANGE 3


Facilities certified to ISO/IEC 17025, General requirements for competence of testing and
calibration laboratories, are considered to have met the requirements of section A.5.


# B.6 [FOCR 408] The certification panel shall perform an on-site inspection to ensure that the


test facility can perform the methods from receipt of a material to issuance of a final report.


# B.7 [FOCR 409] The certification panel shall recommend to the NASA Office of the Chief


Engineer certification of the test facility on the basis of compliance with this document.


The NASA Office of the Chief Engineer has the authority to certify the test facility.


# B.8 [FOCR 410] The certification panel and test facility shall maintain certification records.


# B.9 [FOCR 411] NASA certification of a test facility shall be maintained by meeting the


following requirements:


a. [FOCR 412] The test facility shall perform the required test method(s) for which it is


certified at least once in an 18-month period from its certification date and report the data to
MAPTIS.


b. [FOCR 413] The test facility shall have participated with other NASA-certified test


facilities in the most recent round-robin testing for the test method.


c. [FOCR 414] Round-robin tests for Test 1, Test 4, and Test 7 shall be conducted at


least every 2 years.


Round-robin tests are recommended for other tests in this NASA Technical Standard.


# d. [FOCR 415] The test data shall have been reviewed and approved by the certification


panel or the panel's designees.


# e. [FOCR 416] Facility test procedures shall be available for audit by the certification panel.


B.10 [FOCR 417] All instrumentation used in the test shall be in current calibration and, where


available, bear the appropriate documentation to validate traceability to the appropriate national,
international, or intrinsic measurement standards.


B.11 [FOCR 418] The test facility shall ensure and certify that all testing is accomplished in


accordance with approved test plans and procedures and that the data records and test results are
complete and accurate.


B.12 [FOCR 419] Complete test records shall be prepared by the test facility for each material


or system tested.

# APPENDIX D


# SUBMITTING MATERIAL FOR TESTING


# D.1 Purpose and/or Scope


The purpose of this Appendix is to provide requirements for submitting materials for testing.


The quality of the data obtained from the test material or assembled article is dependent on the
proper identification and packaging of the material/assembled article for submission for test.


Failure to follow good practice may result in unnecessary delays or a loss in confidence in the
data obtained.


# D.2 Submitting Material


# D.2.1 [FOCR 422] The test material shall be free of flaws and visual contamination.


1. The minimum quantity of material to be submitted for each test is shown in table 3.


D.2.3 Solid material should be packaged in a visibly clean, sealed bag (for example, zip-locked,


heat-sealed, taped) that is compatible with the specimen material. The test material name, as
specified by the manufacturer, should appear on the exterior of the bag; the material itself
should not be marked. Non-solid materials may be packaged in the original manufacturing
container.


D.2.4 Electrostatic-discharge-sensitive items and hardware should be packaged in accordance


with their end-use specification.


# D.2.5 Documentation


# a. [FOCR 423] The MSDS shall be provided.


b. [FOCR 424] If the test facility is to prepare material specimens, the manufacturer's


literature or preparation instructions, at a minimum, shall be provided.


# c. [FOCR 425] A completed test request form (section D.3 in this NASA Technical Standard) shall be submitted.


Table 6, Key for Completing "Check Test(s) to be Performed" Section, provides a key for
completing the Test Information section of the test request form.

  <td>4. Evaluation and of Materials</td>

 <tr>
  <th>4.1a</th>
  <td>Material<br/>Evaluation<br/>Requirements</td>
  <td>[FOCR 7] Materials located in habitable areas of spacecraft, including the materials of the spacecraft,<br/>stowed equipment, and experiments, shall be evaluated for flammability and offgassing.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <th>4.1b</th>
  <td>Material<br/>Evaluation</td>
  <td>[FOCR 8] All materials located in other areas shall be evaluated for flammability.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <th>4.1c</th>
  <td>Material<br/>Evaluation</td>
  <td>[FOCR 9] Materials that are exposed to LOX, GOX, and other reactive fluids shall be evaluated for<br/>compatibility with the fluid in their use application.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <th>4.1d</th>
  <td>Material<br/>Evaluation<br/>Requirements</td>
  <td>[FOCR 10] Materials exposed to pressurized breathing gases also shall be evaluated for offgassing.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <th>4.1e</th>
  <td>Material<br/>Evaluation</td>
  <td>[FOCR 11] Electrical insulation materials also shall be evaluated for arc tracking.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <th>4.1f</th>
  <td>Material<br/>Evaluation<br/>Requirements</td>
  <td>[FOCR 12] The worst-case anticipated use environment (most hazardous pressure, temperature, material<br/>thickness, and fluid exposure conditions) shall be used in the evaluation of material suitability.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <th>4.2a</th>
  <td>Materials Usage<br/>Agreement<br/>(MUA)</td>
  <td>[FOCR 13] Systems containing materials that have not been tested or do not meet the criteria of the<br/>required tests shall be verified to be acceptable in the use configuration by analysis or configurational<br/>testing through the use of an MUA.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <th>4.2b</th>
  <td>Materials Usage<br/>Agreement<br/>(MUA)</td>
  <td>[FOCR 14] An MUA shall be prepared in accordance with the MUA system described in NASA-STD-<br/>6016, Standard Materials and Processes Requirements for Spacecraft, for flight hardware, or the latest<br/>version of NASA-STD-5005, Standard for the Design and Fabrication of Ground Support Equipment, for<br/>GSE.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <th>4.2c</th>
  <td>Materials Usage<br/>Agreement</td>
  <td>[FOCR 15] The MUA shall document the acceptance rationale.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <th>4.2d</th>
  <td>Materials Usage<br/>Agreement<br/>(MUA)</td>
  <td>[FOCR 16] The MUA shall be submitted for approval as described in NASA-STD-6016 in accordance<br/>with the Program/Project Materials and Processes Selection, Control, and Implementation Plan.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
</table>

  <td>[FOCR 25] A system flammability evaluation shall be conducted if materials that fail to meet the<br/>criteria of Test 1 are proposed for use in spacecraft, contractually specified test facilities, or<br/>contractually specified GSE.</td>

  <td>[FOCR 26] If the analytical approach is used, the following fire control guidelines shall be addressed<br/>and the results documented and submitted to the responsible NASA program/project M&amp;P organization<br/>for evaluation:<br/>a. The amount and arrangement of flammable materials are controlled to prevent the spread of fire<br/>in the vehicle; any potential fire is isolated in defined areas by firebreaks or other techniques (described<br/>in JSC 29353), without propagation paths to other areas.<br/>b. The design ensures that ignition sources are eliminated or controlled.<br/>c. The effects of an isolated fire on personnel in spacecraft crew-habitable modules are<br/>considered.<br/>d. The rate and magnitude of pressure and temperature increase in any potential fire will not cause<br/>structural to or compromise the pressure of the vehicle.</td>

6.3.3a [FOCR 31] The need for an OCA shall be addressed for compressed air systems and pressurized systems
containing enriched oxygen (greater than 21 percent oxygen by volume).


6.3.3b [FOCR 32] If it is determined that an OCA is not required, concurrence shall be obtained from the
responsible program/project M&P organization.


[FOCR 28] If the results of the evaluation are inconclusive and a major assembly flammability test, such
as a crew module test, is required, the hardware developer shall submit a test plan to the responsible
NASA program/project M&P organization for approval before test.


[FOCR 29] Materials, components, and systems used in LOX and GOX environments shall be evaluated
using the approach shown in figure 1, Approach for Selecting Materials, Components, and Systems used
in LOX and GOX Environments.


[FOCR 30] If the materials are determined to be flammable, an OCA shall be performed in accordance
with NASA/TM-2007-213740, Guide for Oxygen Compatibility Assessments on Oxygen Components
and Systems.

  <th>7.1.4</th>
  <td>Test Method</td>
  <td>[FOCR 65] The test method defined in ISO 14624-1:2003, Space systems - Safety and compatibility of<br/>materials - Part 1: Determination of upward flammability of materials, shall be followed for this test,<br/>with the following exceptions, clarifications, and additions:<br/>The relevant ISO 14624-1:2003 section is included in parentheses.<br/>a. Exception (throughout): One test shall consist of five replicate, identical specimen ignitions.<br/>The term "specimen" is used to indicate one standard-sized section of a material used for<br/>testing. The Upward Flame Propagation Test requires five specimens to produce one standard<br/>test.<br/>b. Clarification (section 3.1): Burn length: the distance from the bottom of the specimen to the<br/>farthest evidence of damage to the test specimen caused by flame consumption as determined by visual<br/>observation, post-test examination, video of burn, and/or other means.<br/>c. Addition (section 3.8): Maximum Oxygen Concentration: The maximum oxygen concentration<br/>at which at least five specimens pass the acceptance criteria of this test.<br/>d. Exception, addition (section 4):<br/>(1) In addition, the ignited specimens shall not ignite the paper (produce flaming combustion)<br/>below the test specimens, which would indicate that the transfer of burning debris would<br/>have sufficient energy to ignite adjacent materials.<br/>(2) If, during a test, the paper ignites because of burning debris, subsequent burns during the<br/>same material test shall be conducted without the paper.<br/>This is done because the burning paper can inhibit the burning of the test specimen. Individual<br/>specimen test results performed without the paper should be identified in the test report.<br/>Failure of any one specimen constitutes failure of the material in that test environment.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
</table>

  <th>7.4.4</th>
  <td>Test Method</td>
  <td>[FOCR 72] The test method described in ISO 14624-2:2003, Space systems - Safety and compatibility<br/>of materials - Part 2: Determination of flammability of electrical-wire insulation and accessory<br/>materials, shall be followed for this test, with the following exceptions, clarifications, and additions:<br/>The relevant ISO 14624-2:2003 sections are included in parentheses.<br/>a. Exception (throughout): One test shall consist of five replicate, identical specimen ignitions.<br/>b. Exception (throughout): The minimum quantity of material required for testing is listed in table 3<br/>of this document.<br/>c. Clarification (section 4.1): Burn length: the distance from the bottom of the specimen as it sits in<br/>the test stand to the farthest evidence of damage to the specimen's test insulation material caused by<br/>flame consumption as determined by visual observation, post-test examination, video of burn, and/or<br/>other means.<br/>d. Addition (section 4.3): Transfer of burning debris: movement of burning particles from a burning<br/>specimen to adjacent materials with sufficient energy to ignite the paper below the test specimen.<br/>e. Addition (section 4): Maximum Oxygen Concentration: The maximum oxygen concentration at<br/>which at least five specimens pass the acceptance criteria of this test.<br/>f. Exception (section 6.1): A standard test shall consist of five wire specimens tested.<br/>g. Addition (section 6.1): In addition, the ignited wire test specimens shall not ignite the paper<br/>(produce flaming combustion) below the test specimens, which would indicate that the transfer of<br/>burning debris would have sufficient energy to ignite adjacent materials.</td>

It does not include compounds that have adequate spectral information but are labeled as<br/>unidentified components because suitable standard spectra for identification are not available.<br/>d. Clarification (section 6.1): The test atmosphere should be at least a volume fraction of 20.9 (±2)<br/>percent for GOX with the balance GN2 or argon, and the test pressure should be ±15 kPa (±2.18 psi) of<br/>the test facility ambient pressure.<br/>h. Addition (section 6.1 note): Batteries or assembled articles containing batteries should be tested<br/>in an inert atmosphere to reduce the risk of generating an explosive gas mixture. The batteries or<br/>assembled articles containing batteries tested in an inert atmosphere do not need to be tested again in an<br/>oxygen atmosphere for toxic offgassing.<br/>i. Clarification, addition (section 7.3): Analytical instrumentation is not specified; however, it<br/>should be capable of the identification and quantification of all offgassed products at, or below, their<br/>SMAC concentrations when tested at a test-material-mass-to-specimen-container-volume ratio of 5.0<br/>(±0.25) g/l.<br/>(1) If the instrumentation cannot achieve this sensitivity, the minimum reportable concentration<br/>(reporting limit) for those offgassed products (except for formaldehyde) shall be reported.<br/>(2) For formaldehyde, the analytical technique shall be capable of detecting a concentration of<br/>0.1 ppm or current SMAC.<br/>The recommended analytical instruments include a gas chromatograph, primarily using a flame<br/>ionization detector, a gas chromatograph/mass spectrometer, and an infrared spectrophotometer. Some<br/>analytical compounds may be more difficult to determine; therefore, special methods may be required to<br/>identify and quantify these compounds. For example, the determination of formaldehyde may be<br/>performed using the proposed method of trapping on 2,4-dinitrophenylhydrazine cartridges for<br/>derivation and subsequent analysis by High Performance Liquid Chromatography.<br/>In some cases, the reporting limit for a compound is greater than the SMAC, but the ability to meet the<br/>SMAC requirement is known to be crucial. To determine if the compound offgasses at the SMAC, it is<br/>necessary to test more than the standard test-material-mass-to-specimen-container-volume ratio of 5.0<br/>(±0.25) g/l. The quantity of test material should be increased proportionally from the standard test-<br/>material-mass-to-specimen-container-volume ratio to a quantity that will allow the analysis to meet the<br/>SMAC requirement.</td>

 <tr>
  <th>7.17.4</th>
  <td>Test Method</td>
  <td>[[FOCR 166] The test method defined in ASTM G124, Standard Test Method for Determining the<br/>Combustion Behavior of Metallic Materials in Oxygen-Enriched Atmospheres, shall be followed for this<br/>test, with the following exceptions, clarifications, and additions.<br/>The relevant ASTM G124 section is included in parentheses.<br/>ISO 14624-4, Space systems - Safety and compatibility of materials - Part 4: Determination of upward<br/>flammability of materials in pressurized GOX or oxygen-enriched environments, describes a similar test<br/>procedure.<br/>a. Exception (throughout): The specimen shall be tested at ambient temperature if its use<br/>temperature is ≤260 °C (≤500 °F).<br/>b. Exception (section 8.2): For metallic materials, the promoter shall consist of a sufficient quantity<br/>of aluminum or magnesium to release at least 3.0 kJ (717 cal).<br/>c. Exception (section 8.2): For polymeric materials, the promoter shall be sufficient for ignition of<br/>the material, as determined by the responsible NASA program/project M&amp;P organization.<br/>d. Exception (section 10.1): The test specimens shall be a minimum of 10.2 cm (4 in) (±5 percent)<br/>in length.<br/>A minimum length of 15 cm (6 in) (±5 percent) is preferable.<br/>e. Exception (section 10.1): If a material cannot be obtained or prepared in the cylindrical form, a<br/>nonstandard specimen shall be prepared.<br/>The nonstandard specimen should have 0.32-cm (0.126-in) (±5 percent) sides and should be a<br/>minimum of 10.2 cm (4 in) (±5 percent) in length.<br/>f. Addition (section 16): The sample temperature measurement shall have precision of ±5 percent<br/>of reading.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
</table>

  <td>7.18 Arc Tracking (Dry Arc Propagation Resistance) (Test 18)</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <td>7.18.3a</td>
  <td>Test Criteria</td>
  <td>[FOCR 167] To determine the arc propagation resistance of the wire configuration, at least 15 seven-wire<br/>bundles shall be tested.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <td>7.18.3b</td>
  <td>Test Criteria</td>
  <td>[FOCR 168] A wire insulation material shall be considered to have met the acceptance criteria of this test<br/>if all the following requirements are met:</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <td>7.18.3b(1)</td>
  <td>Test Criteria</td>
  <td>[FOCR 169] At least 64 wires shall pass the dielectric test.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <td>7.18.3b(2)</td>
  <td>Test Criteria</td>
  <td>[FOCR 170] Three wires or less shall fail the dielectric test in any one bundle.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <td>7.18.3b(3)</td>
  <td>Test Criteria</td>
  <td>[FOCR 171] Actual damage to the wire shall not be more than 7.6 cm (3 in) in any one bundle.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <td>7.18.5</td>
  <td>Test Conditions</td>
  <td>[FOCR 172] Test conditions shall be as defined in either MIL-STD-2223, Test Methods for Insulated<br/>Wire, Method 3007, Dry Arc-Propagation Resistance, or SAE AS4373, Test Methods for Insulated<br/>Wire, Method 508, Dry Arc Propagation Resistance.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <td>7.18.6a</td>
  <td>Specimen</td>
  <td>[FOCR 173] A test specimen shall be a bundle of seven wires and of sufficient length (35.6 cm (14 in)<br/>to allow the bundle to be installed in the test fixture.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <td>7.18.6b</td>
  <td>Specimen</td>
  <td>[FOCR 174] Fifteen bundles shall be required for a full test.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <td>7.18.6c</td>
  <td>Specimen</td>
  <td>[FOCR 175] The specimens shall be prepared according to MIL-STD-2223, Method 3007, or SAE<br/>AS4373, Method 508.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <td>7.18.7</td>
  <td>Test System</td>
  <td>[FOCR 176] The test system shall be that described in MIL-STD-2223, Method 3007, or SAE AS4373,<br/>Method 508.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <td>7.18.8</td>
  <td>Pre-Test<br/>Procedure</td>
  <td>[FOCR 177] The pre-test procedures shall be those described in MIL-STD-2223, Method 3007, or SAE<br/>AS4373, Method 508.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
</table>

  <td>Test Procedure</td>
  <td>[FOCR 178] The test procedures shall be those described in MIL-STD-2223, Method 3007, or SAE<br/>AS4373, Method 508.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <th>7.18.10a</th>
  <td>Reporting</td>
  <td>[FOCR 179] The test report shall include specimen identification, test conditions, total number of wires<br/>that pass the dielectric test, number of wires from each bundle that pass the dielectric test, and the length<br/>of to each individual wire in the bundle.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>

  <th>7.18.10b</th>
  <td>Reporting</td>
  <td>[FOCR 180] The test report shall be submitted to MAPTIS in a MAPTIS-compatible format.</td>

  <th></th>
  <td></td>
  <td>Appendix A Supplemental Tests</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <th>A.2a</th>
  <td>Test Methods for<br/>Providing<br/>Supplemental<br/>Data</td>
  <td>[FOCR 181] Tests shall be performed by test facilities certified in accordance with Appendix B in this<br/>NASA Technical Standard.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <th>A.2a(1)</th>
  <td>Test Methods for<br/>Providing<br/>Supplemental<br/>Data</td>
  <td>[FOCR 182] The test facility shall demonstrate GLPs to produce accurate and repeatable test data.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <th>A.2a(2)</th>
  <td>Test Methods for<br/>Providing<br/>Supplemental<br/>Data</td>
  <td>[FOCR 183] GLPs shall include calibration and maintenance procedures.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <th>A.2a(3)</th>
  <td>Test Methods for<br/>Providing<br/>Supplemental<br/>Data</td>
  <td>[FOCR 184] At least every 2 years, test facilities shall demonstrate testing proficiency to maintain<br/>certification in accordance with Appendix B in this NASA Technical Standard.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <th>A.2b</th>
  <td>Test Methods for<br/>Providing<br/>Supplemental<br/>Data</td>
  <td>[FOCR 185] Properly identified material for testing shall be provided by the responsible NASA Center or<br/>contractor hardware supplier and accompanied by a test request form similar to that provided in<br/>Appendix D in this NASA Technical Standard.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <th>A.2c(1)</th>
  <td>Test Methods for<br/>Providing<br/>Supplemental<br/>Data</td>
  <td>[FOCR 186] Materials shall be accompanied by MSDSs to comply with materials-handling requirements<br/>defined by OSHA.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <th>A.2c(2)</th>
  <td>Material and<br/>Specimen<br/>Receipt</td>
  <td>[FOCR 187] Material specimens shall be visually inspected and any flaws or contamination noted in the<br/>test report.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
</table>

  <td>[FOCR 188] Specimens shall be prepared in the proper dimensions.</td>
  <td></td>

  <th>A.2c(4)</th>

  <td></td>

 <tr>
  <th>A.2c(5)</th>
  <td>Material and<br/>Specimen<br/>Receipt<br/>and</td>
  <td>[FOCR 190] Specimens shall be cleaned to end-use specifications.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <th>A.2c(6)</th>
  <td>Material and<br/>Specimen<br/>Receipt<br/>and Preparation</td>
  <td>[FOCR 191] Specimens shall be inspected after preparation to ensure suitability for the specific test<br/>requested.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <th>A.2c(7)</th>
  <td>Material and<br/>Specimen<br/>Receipt<br/>and</td>
  <td>[FOCR 192] As a minimum, all fluids used for testing shall meet or exceed user specifications.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <th>A.2d</th>
  <td>Material and<br/>Specimen<br/>Receipt<br/>and Preparation</td>
  <td>[FOCR 193] When there is a deviation from standard test parameters, e.g., nonstandard specimen<br/>preparation or test conditions, the test shall be identified as nonstandard.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <th>A.2.1.3</th>
  <td>Test Criteria</td>
  <td>[FOCR 194] The test shall be conducted on specimens that are representative of those in the intended use<br/>environment and at worst-case conditions.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <th>A.2.1.5</th>
  <td>Test Conditions</td>
  <td>[FOCR 195] Test conditions (incident heat flux and oxygen concentration) shall simulate the worst-case<br/>conditions in which and combustion of the material are most to occur.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <th>A.2.1.6a</th>
  <td>Specimen<br/>Preparation</td>
  <td>[FOCR 196] Specimens shall be prepared in accordance with ASTM E1354, Standard Test Method for<br/>Heat and Visible Smoke Release Rates for Materials and Products Using an Oxygen Consumption<br/>Calorimeter.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <th>A.2.1.6b</th>
  <td>Specimen</td>
  <td>[FOCR 197] Coatings or any viscous materials that cannot be mounted for testing without a substrate<br/>shall be to the end-use substrate material in the worst-case thickness.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
</table>

  <td></td>

  <th>Section</th>
  <td>Description</td>

 <tr>
  <th>A.2.1.6c(1)</th>
  <td>Specimen<br/>Preparation,<br/>Cleaning of</td>
  <td>[FOCR 198] Contamination on the surfaces of solid, nonporous specimens shall be removed by washing<br/>with deionized water and any detergent, rinsing with deionized water, and drying with filtered GN2.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <th>A.2.1.6c(2)</th>
  <td>Specimen<br/>Preparation,<br/>Cleaning of</td>
  <td>[FOCR 199] Particulate on the surfaces of solid, porous specimens shall be removed with filtered GN2.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <th>A.2.1.7</th>
  <td>Test System</td>
  <td>[FOCR 200] The test system shall be as described in ASTM E1354 but with additional capabilities<br/>that will allow in GOX/GN2 mixtures other than air.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <th>A.2.1.8</th>
  <td>Pre-Test<br/>Procedure</td>
  <td>[FOCR 201] The pre-test procedures shall be as described in ASTM E1354.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <th>A.2.1.9</th>
  <td>Test Procedure</td>
  <td>[FOCR 202] The test procedures shall be as described in ASTM E1354, with the following exceptions:<br/>a. The specimen shall be placed on the load cell in the test chamber and exposed to the test<br/>atmosphere by allowing the test gases to flow through the test chamber.<br/>b. The initial concentration shall be measured.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <th>A.2.1.10</th>
  <td>Reporting</td>
  <td>[FOCR 203] The test report content shall be as described in ASTM E1354.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <th>A.2.2.5</th>
  <td>Selection of a<br/>Flash Point Test<br/>Method</td>
  <td>[FOCR 204] The appropriate flash point test method for the application shall be selected by following the<br/>recommendations of ASTM E502, Standard Test Method for Selection and Use of ASTM Standards for<br/>the Determination of Flash Point of Chemicals by Closed Cup Methods.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <th>A.2.2.6</th>
  <td>Test Systems and<br/>Procedures</td>
  <td>[FOCR 205] The test systems and procedures shall be identical to those described in the selected ASTM<br/>test</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <th>A.2.2.7</th>
  <td>Reporting</td>
  <td>[FOCR 206] The test report shall include specimen identification, the method used, test conditions, the<br/>flash point of the liquid corrected to 101.3 kPa (14.7 psia), and observations from the test.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <th>A.2.2.8</th>
  <td>Precision</td>
  <td>[FOCR 207] Measurements shall meet the precision requirements of the ASTM method(s) used.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <th>A.2.3.4a</th>
  <td>Test Conditions</td>
  <td>[FOCR 208] The test atmosphere shall be at least a volume fraction of 20.9 (±2) percent for GOX, with<br/>the balance GN2 or</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <th>A.2.3.4b</th>
  <td>Test Conditions</td>
  <td>[FOCR 209] Test pressure shall be ±3.5 kPa (±0.5 psi) of the test facility ambient pressure.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <th>A.2.3.4c</th>
  <td>Test Conditions</td>
  <td>[FOCR 210] The maximum volume fraction limits (expressed as a volume fraction in µl/l) for impurities<br/>in the compressed gases shall be:<br/>(1) Carbon monoxide: 1.0.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
</table>

  <th>A.2.3.5a</th>

  <td></td>

 <tr>
  <th>A.2.3.5b</th>
  <td>Specimen</td>
  <td>[FOCR 213] Handling of test specimens shall be in a manner that preserves the integrity of the specimen<br/>surface without contaminants.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <th>A.2.3.5c</th>
  <td>Specimen</td>
  <td>[FOCR 214] All materials shall meet the requirement of test-material-mass-to-specimen-container-<br/>volume ratio of 5.0</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <th>A.2.3.5d(1)A</th>
  <td>Specimen<br/>Preparation</td>
  <td>[FOCR 215] Materials that are essentially two-dimensional and require application to a substrate (for<br/>example, coatings, primers, inks, paints, adhesives, tapes, and thin film lubricants) shall be applied at<br/>their thickness of use to clean aluminum substrates.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <th>A.2.3.5d(1)Ai</th>
  <td>Specimen</td>
  <td>[FOCR 216] A sufficient number of substrates with applied specimen material shall be prepared to<br/>a net ratio of 5.0</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <th>A.2.3.5d(1)Aii</th>
  <td>Specimen</td>
  <td>[FOCR 217] The approximate total specimen surface area shall be recorded.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <th>A.2.3.5d(1)B</th>
  <td>Specimen<br/>Preparation</td>
  <td>[FOCR 218] Materials that are essentially two-dimensional and are not applied to a substrate (for<br/>example, fabrics, photographic film plastic, plastic film, elastomers, and non-adhesive tape) shall be cut<br/>to convenient test dimensions.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <th>A.2.3.5d(1)Bi</th>
  <td>Specimen</td>
  <td>[FOCR 219] Heat-shrinkable tubing shall be shrunk to simulate actual use configuration.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <th>A.2.3.5d(1)Bii</th>
  <td>Specimen</td>
  <td>[FOCR 220] A sufficient quantity of specimens shall be prepared to provide a test-material-mass-to-<br/>ratio of 5.0</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <th>A.2.3.5d(1)C</th>
  <td>Specimen</td>
  <td>[FOCR 221] Non-curing liquids shall be placed in suitable non-reactive vessels and the approximate total<br/>surface area</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <th>A.2.3.5d(1)D<br/>A.2.3.5d(1)Di</th>
  <td>Specimen<br/>Preparation</td>
  <td>Specialized items and materials that do not meet the above requirements and that require special<br/>handling, e.g., non-homogeneous materials.<br/>[FOCR 222] Such materials shall be tested in the manner designated by the responsible NASA<br/>M&amp;P</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
</table>

  <td>Specimen</td>
  <td>[FOCR 223] The manner of testing and specimen preparation shall be reported.</td>

  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <th>A.2.3.5d(2)A</th>
  <td>Specimen<br/>Preparation<br/>for Flight<br/>Articles</td>

  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <th>A.2.3.5d(2)Ai</th>
  <td>Specimen<br/>Preparation<br/>for Flight<br/>Articles</td>
  <td>[FOCR 225] These items shall be removed before testing.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <th>A.2.3.5d(2)Aii</th>
  <td>Specimen<br/>Preparation<br/>for Flight<br/>Articles</td>
  <td>[FOCR 226] The absence of items (for example, batteries or photographic film) that will be included<br/>during flight but that are not included with the specimen shall be recorded.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <th>A.2.3.5d(2)B</th>
  <td>Specimen<br/>Preparation<br/>for Flight<br/>Articles</td>

  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <th>A.2.3.5d(2)C</th>
  <td>Specimen<br/>Preparation<br/>for Flight<br/>Articles</td>
  <td>[FOCR 228] When the offgas test atmosphere is preserved for odor assessment, the odor assessment shall<br/>be initiated within 14 days from the start of the specimen gas preservation.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <th>A.2.3.5e</th>
  <td>Specimen</td>
  <td>[FOCR 229] Specimens shall be cleaned in accordance with section 8.4 of ISO 14624-3:2005.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <th>A.2.3.6a</th>
  <td>Test System</td>
  <td>[FOCR 230] The test system shall consist of the specimen container, the test chamber with controlled<br/>temperature, and volunteer odor panel personnel.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <th>A.2.3.6b</th>
  <td>Test System</td>
  <td>[FOCR 231] The specimen container, including any soft goods, shall have an odor rating of 0<br/>(Undetectable), as defined in section A.2.3.8.g in this NASA Technical Standard, when an empty<br/>chamber is tested in accordance with the in section A.2.3.8 in this NASA Technical Standard.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <th>A.2.3.6c</th>
  <td>Test System</td>
  <td>[FOCR 232] The test chamber shall have the capability to maintain the test temperature to within ±3 °C<br/>for the duration of the test.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <th>A.2.3.6d</th>
  <td>Test System</td>
  <td>[FOCR 233] The test chamber instrumentation shall have the capability to record the temperature</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>

  <th>A.2.3.6e</th>
  <td>Test System</td>
  <td>[FOCR 234] Glass syringes (30 cm3 (1.8 in3) capacity) shall be used for measuring and administering the<br/>odor</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <th>A.2.3.6f</th>
  <td>Test System</td>
  <td>[FOCR 235] When administering the specimen, a low-odor non-obtrusive mask that covers the nose<br/>and mouth shall be used.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <th>A.2.3.6g</th>
  <td>Test System</td>
  <td>[FOCR 236] The syringes and masks shall have an odor rating of 0 (Undetectable), as defined in section<br/>A2.3.8.g in this NASA Technical Standard, when an empty chamber or blank air specimen is tested in<br/>accordance with the in section A.2.3.8 in this NASA Technical Standard.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <th>A.2.3.7a</th>
  <td>Pre-Test<br/>Procedure</td>
  <td>[FOCR 237] A NASA or NASA contractor volunteer shall perform the following:<br/>(1) Read, understand, and sign a consent form as required in NPR 7100.1, Protection of Human<br/>Research Subjects, and NPD 7100.8, Protection of Human Research Subjects.<br/>(2) Be examined and qualified by a registered medical professional.<br/>(3) Be odor qualified by distinguishing the seven primary standards (table 5, Seven Primary<br/>Standards for Detection of from three odorless standards.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <th>A.2.3.7b</th>
  <td>Pre-Test<br/>Procedure</td>
  <td>[FOCR 238] An odor panel shall test each new compressed gas that is used for test atmospheres before<br/>use.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <th>A.2.3.7c</th>
  <td>Pre-Test<br/>Procedure</td>
  <td>[FOCR 239] Gases used for test atmospheres shall be judged as being odorless.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <th>A.2.3.7d</th>
  <td>Pre-Test<br/>Procedure</td>
  <td>[FOCR 240] The specimen containers shall be cleaned by washing with a diluted soap solution and<br/>with deionized allowed to and then with filtered air or GN2.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <th>A.2.3.7e</th>
  <td>Pre-Test<br/>Procedure</td>
  <td>[FOCR 241] Before use, each container shall be certified to be free of odor by the odor panel.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <th>A.2.3.7f</th>
  <td>Pre-Test<br/>Procedure</td>
  <td>[FOCR 242] Before odor testing, materials or assembled articles shall be tested for offgassed products</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <th>A.2.3.7g</th>
  <td>Pre-Test<br/>Procedure</td>
  <td>[FOCR 243] If the gas from the specimen container has been determined to be unsafe to test, based on<br/>the offgassing analysis, the calculated toxic level and reasons for not performing the test shall be<br/>recorded.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <th>A.2.3.7h</th>
  <td>Pre-Test<br/>Procedure</td>
  <td>[FOCR 244] Duplicate specimens of materials shall be offgassed-one specimen for offgassing analysis<br/>and the other for odor assessment.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <th>A.2.3.7i</th>
  <td>Pre-Test<br/>Procedure</td>
  <td>[FOCR 245] If the specimen cannot be provided in duplicate, the same specimen shall be used in both<br/>offgassing analysis and odor assessment.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <th>A.2.3.7j</th>
  <td>Pre-Test<br/>Procedure</td>
  <td>[FOCR 246] Before each test, members of the odor panel shall receive a nose and throat examination<br/>nasal irritation or other abnormal a medical staff member.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
</table>

 <tr>
  <th>A.2.3.7k</th>
  <td>Pre-Test<br/>Procedure</td>
  <td>[FOCR 247] Results of the examination shall be recorded.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <th>A.2.3.7l</th>
  <td>Pre-Test<br/>Procedure</td>
  <td>[FOCR 248] Each odor panel member shall identify an odorless solution from two of the seven primary<br/>standards.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <th>A.2.3.7m</th>
  <td>Pre-Test<br/>Procedure</td>
  <td>[FOCR 249] Members of the odor panel shall not be allowed to participate if their sense of smell has<br/>been affected in any manner, such as by recent smoking, ingestion of highly flavored foods, or<br/>to</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <th>A.2.3.8a</th>
  <td>Test Procedure</td>
  <td>[FOCR 250] The specimen shall be weighed and placed in the specimen container.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <th>A.2.3.8b</th>
  <td>Test Procedure</td>
  <td>[FOCR 251] The atmosphere in the specimen container shall be replaced with the test atmosphere, either<br/>or evacuation.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <th>A.2.3.8b(1)</th>
  <td>Test Procedure</td>
  <td>[FOCR 252] The requesting organization shall indicate if the specimen can or cannot withstand a<br/>vacuum.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <th>A.2.3.8b(2)</th>
  <td>Test Procedure</td>
  <td>[FOCR 253] Exposure of any specimen to vacuum shall be less than 3 min.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <th>A.2.3.8c</th>
  <td>Test Procedure</td>
  <td>[FOCR 254] The specimen container, with the test atmosphere, shall be at the specified test pressure<br/>when the test is achieved.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <th>A.2.3.8d</th>
  <td>Test Procedure</td>
  <td>[FOCR 255] The specimen shall be placed in the test chamber and heated to the test temperature of 50<br/>°C unless otherwise</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <th>A.2.3.8d(1)</th>
  <td>Test Procedure</td>
  <td>[FOCR 256] The test temperature shall be maintained for 72 (±1) hr.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <th>A.2.3.8d(2)</th>
  <td>Test Procedure</td>
  <td>[FOCR 257] Then, the specimen container shall be cooled to ambient temperature and the pressure<br/>recorded.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <th>A.2.3.8e</th>
  <td>Test Procedure</td>
  <td>[FOCR 258] The odor panel shall be convened.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <th>A.2.3.8f</th>
  <td>Test Procedure</td>
  <td>[FOCR 259] At least five qualified members shall be administered at least 30 cm3 of the gas from the<br/>container.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <th>A.2.3.8g</th>
  <td>Test Procedure</td>
  <td>[FOCR 260] Using the following scale, each odor panel member shall assign an odor characteristic to the<br/>gas from the specimen container:<br/>Undetectable: 0<br/>Barely Detectable: 1<br/>Easily Detectable: 2<br/>Objectionable: 3<br/>Revolting: 4</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
 <tr>
  <th>A.2.3.8h</th>
  <td>Test Procedure</td>
  <td>[FOCR 261] Between 2 and 4 hr after the test, the panel members shall be examined medically for nasal<br/>irritation or other abnormal conditions and the results of this examination recorded.</td>
  <td></td>
  <td></td>
 </tr>
</table>

 <tr>

  <th>Section</th>
  <td>Description</td>
